### [MODIFICADO] `microsoft_vscode-docs::release-notes/v1_145.md`
- **URL Canónica**: https://code.visualstudio.com/updates/v1_145
- **Hash Anterior**: `8cfae382c803d4064a017bcca4910b39dd7c16b1d7fb650374137f71d5822502`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `c97cadedb6ccd001a8539cfa7b7f56caada4c0363a7132e6165dc5eff919f823`

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::release-notes/v1_145.md (prev: 8cfae382)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::release-notes/v1_145.md (new: c97caded)
@@ -1,76 +1,348 @@
-**MetaDescription**: Learn what is new in Visual Studio Code 1.145 (Insiders).
+**MetaDescription**: Learn what's new in Visual Studio Code 1.145
 
 # Visual Studio Code 1.145
 
-Follow us on [LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/showcase/vs-code), [X](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=533687), [Bluesky](https://bsky.app/profile/vscode.dev), [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/vscode)
+## Release highlights
+
+This week, your remote agent sessions feel a bit more local. Preview apps and use canvases right in VS Code, fine-tune automation schedules with cron, and turn down the noise in chat. And when you need a break, Blobby is ready to play.
+
+* [Remote agents](#remote-agents): Preview apps and use interactive canvases from remote agent sessions without leaving the Agents window.
+
+* [Automations](#cron-schedules-and-custom-agents-for-local-automations): Run recurring agent tasks on custom schedules beyond the built-in presets with cron.
+
+* [Agent verbosity](#choose-agent-verbosity): Keep chats concise or see the full details of the agent's reasoning and tool calls, your choice.
+
+* [File exclusions](#show-specific-files-in-excluded-folders): Exclude folders while keeping the files you need visible and searchable.
+
+* [Fun](#blobby-jump-game-experimental): Take a short break with a platform-jumping game starring Blobby, the VS Code pet.
+
+## Remote agents
+
+### Browse over remote agent host connections (Preview)
+
+**Setting**: ``workbench.browser.enableRemoteProxy``
+
+When an agent session runs on a [remote agent host](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/remote-agent-sessions), the integrated browser in the Agents window now loads pages through that host, as it already does in [remote workspaces](https://code.visualstudio.com/updates/v1_125#_browse-over-remote-connections-preview). When the agent starts a dev server on the remote machine, you can open it right in the Agents window to preview the app, validate the agent's changes, and give it feedback, as you would in a local session.
+
+![Screenshot showing the URL bar in the integrated browser, showing 'Connected via kyles-mbp'.](images/1_145/remote-browser.webp)
+
+Enable ``workbench.browser.enableRemoteProxy`` to try it out, and file any issues you encounter in the [VS Code repository](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/issues).
+
+### Remote canvases (Preview)
+
+**Settings**: ``workbench.browser.enableRemoteProxy``, ``chat.canvases.enabled``
+
+Thanks to the remote agent host connections support, you can now also use [canvases](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/canvases) that are hosted on a remote agent host.
+
+Enable ``chat.canvases.enabled`` and ``workbench.browser.enableRemoteProxy`` to try it out, and file any issues you encounter in the [VS Code repository](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/issues).
+
+## Agents
+
+### Mix and match agent harnesses in one session (Preview)
+
+**Setting**: ``chat.agentHost.multipleChatProviders.enabled``
+
+[Nested sessions](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions#_run-multiple-chats-in-a-session) let you keep related conversations together within a single main chat session. Previously, every nested session inherited its parent session's harness. If you wanted a second opinion from another agent, you had to start a separate session and lose that grouping.
+
+In this release, nested sessions can use different harnesses than their parent session. For example, ask Claude to review the plan that a Copilot chat wrote, or give Codex the same task, side by side in the same session. When you create a new nested session, use the harness picker to switch to a different harness.
+
+Each chat has its own model, agent, and permission levels, based on its harness. Changing them in one chat doesn't affect the others. When you move a chat, it keeps its history and continues with its selected harness. When you fork a nested session, it continues with the same configuration.
+
+A chat that uses a different harness than its session names its harness when you hover over its chat tab or its row in the sessions list.
+
+<video src="images/1_145/multi-harness-session.mp4" title="Video showing a Copilot session in the Agents window where Claude and Codex nested sessions are added through the harness picker, each answering with its own model and permissions." autoplay loop controls muted></video>
+
+Enable ``chat.agentHost.multipleChatProviders.enabled`` to get started. Let us know what you think by [filing an issue](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/issues).
+
+### Cron schedules and custom agents for local automations
+
+Instead of manually running agent tasks one by one, you can use [automations](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/automations) to schedule and run them automatically, either on demand or on a set schedule.
+
+This release gives you more flexibility for local automations and keeps the Automations view focused on active work:
+
+* **Use cron schedules:** Select **Cron** to enter a five-field cron expression when the other schedule presets aren't precise enough. The dialog validates the expression and describes the resulting schedule. Cron schedules are not available for cloud automations.
+
+* **Use workspace custom agents:** Choose a custom agent from the selected workspace when you create or edit a local automation.
+
+* **Focus on active automations:** Disabled automations appear in a collapsed **Disabled automations** section, so they don't obscure run history and active automations.
+
+![Screenshot showing the Cron schedule editor for a local automation.](images/1_145/cron-automations.webp)
+
+Learn more about [creating and managing agent automations](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/automations).
+
+### Pin canvases to sidebar (Experimental)
+
+**Setting**: ``chat.canvases.enabled``
+
+To make it easier to access frequently used canvases, you can now pin a canvas to appear in the left sidebar. To pin a canvas, open it and select the **Add to Sidebar** button above the canvas. Pinned canvases remain accessible in the sidebar even when you navigate away from them.
+
+<video src="images/1_145/pinned-canvases.mp4" title="Video showing how to pin a canvas to the sidebar in VS Code." autoplay loop controls muted></video>
+
+### Copilot harness
+
+The Copilot harness continues to be the default for new sessions in VS Code. It's our latest harness, built on the Copilot SDK, a shared foundation that helps align behavior, capabilities, and improvements across Copilot products.
+
+When you start a new session, **Copilot** in the harness picker indicates that the session uses the Copilot harness. In VS Code, you can keep Copilot harness sessions running in the background and access them from both the VS Code editor window and the Agents window. These sessions run through the [Agent Host](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/concepts/agent-host).
+
+To help you adopt the Copilot harness with your existing workflows, we've added a [Local-to-Copilot harness transition guide](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/move-to-copilot). It covers customization compatibility, session continuity, and organization rollout and verification.
+
+Thank you for the feedback that continues to shape these improvements. Please continue [filing issues](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/issues) about your experience, especially if a workflow or integration prevents your organization from moving off the Local harness.
+
+Learn how to [work with the Copilot harness](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/agent-harnesses), or explore the architecture and workflows in the [agent host blog post](https://code.visualstudio.com/blogs/2026/08/26/agent-host-architecture).
+
+### Working set improvements (Experimental)
+
+**Setting**: ``chat.editor.workingSet.enabled``
+
+Last release, we introduced the working set for Copilot harness sessions, letting you review pending agent edits. You can **keep** or **undo** changes, as you could do with the Local harness.
+
+![Screenshot of the working set in Copilot harness sessions.](images/1_145/working-set.webp)
+
+We are further improving the working set experience in Copilot harness sessions:
+
+* Files in WSL, Dev Containers, and SSH workspaces open from the working set on the correct host.
+* When several tool calls edit the same file in parallel, the working set tracks those edits and the editor's inline review controls remain available after editing finishes.
+
+The working set functionality remains enabled by default in the editor window. Learn more about [reviewing agent edits with the working set](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/review-code-edits#_review-pending-edits-with-the-working-set), including which edits it tracks.
+
+### Improved guidance when required sandboxing is unavailable
+
+[Agent sandboxing](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/agent-sandboxing) limits what terminal commands can access, like the file system and network resources. Your organization can require it with managed settings that disallow bypassing the sandbox.
+
+If your organization requires sandboxing but your machine can't run it, the warning banner explains why terminal commands are blocked and how to resolve the problem. It shows the specific cause, such as a missing dependency, and links to troubleshooting steps.
+
+If your account and organization support [cloud sessions (Experimental)](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/cloud-agent-sessions), consider running agent tools in the cloud instead of on your machine.
+
```

### [MODIFICADO] `langgraph::checkpointers`
- **URL Canónica**: https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/checkpointers
- **Hash Anterior**: `d4d8fb4caa84608ae0fb4a2d1080e4bcee3b4bfc02cb9c14f1de1d29db43089c`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `7c0d78599d0410cd8fbfe1ab4373b7fc134565ef6285ee826b5d595e12e0ffc4`

```diff
--- langgraph::checkpointers (prev: d4d8fb4c)
+++ langgraph::checkpointers (new: 7c0d7859)
@@ -45,6 +45,23 @@
 A thread's current and historical state can be retrieved. To persist state, a thread must be created prior to executing a run. The LangSmith API provides several endpoints for creating and managing threads and thread state. See the [API reference](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langsmith/) for more details.
 
 The checkpointer uses `thread_id` as the primary key for storing and retrieving checkpoints. Without it, the checkpointer cannot save state or resume execution after an [interrupt](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md), since the checkpointer uses `thread_id` to load the saved state.
+
+#### Choose how to continue a thread
+
+The `thread_id` identifies saved state. The input and `checkpoint_id` determine whether an invocation starts new work, resumes unfinished work, or replays history. Reusing a thread ID alone does not mean that execution resumes.
+
+With a checkpointer and an existing thread, choose the invocation for your goal:
+
+| Goal | Invocation | Execution |
+| - | - | - |
+| Submit new input | `graph.invoke(input, config)` | Applies the input to saved state and starts from the entry point. |
+| Continue after an error | `graph.invoke(None, config)` | Loads the latest checkpoint and retries unfinished work. Omit `checkpoint_id`. |
+| Answer an `interrupt()` | `graph.invoke(Command(resume=answer), config)` | Supplies the interrupt's return value. The interrupted node starts again from its beginning. |
+| Replay history | `graph.invoke(None, snapshot.config)` | Uses a snapshot from `get_state_history()` and re-executes nodes after that checkpoint. |
+
+New input does not clear the thread. LangGraph applies it using the state's [reducers](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api.md#reducers). Use a new `thread_id` to start with empty state.
+
+Resume returns the saved result without running nodes when the latest checkpoint has no unfinished work. For runnable examples, see [Functional API error recovery](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api-tasks.md#resume-after-an-error), [resuming interrupts](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/interrupts.md#resuming-interrupts), and [replaying history](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md#replay).
 
 ### Checkpoints
 
```

whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="langchain-core-v1-7-0"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#langchain-core-v1-7-0" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer"><code>langchain-core</code> v1.7.0</span></h2><span data-as="p">In <a href="/oss/python/langchain/event-streaming" class="link">event streaming</a>, tool-call arguments now stream as <code>tool-call-delta</code> events. Each event carries only the new part of the arguments, as <code>text-delta</code> does for text. Earlier versions sent <code>block-delta</code> events that repeated all of the arguments so far.</span><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="upgrade-clients-before-the-server"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#upgrade-clients-before-the-server" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Upgrade clients before the server</span></h3><span data-as="p">Clients older than the versions below do not handle <code>tool-call-delta</code>. For example, earlier versions of <code>@langchain/react</code> can stop updating a <code>useMessages</code> view.  <li>Upgrade every client that reads the stream:
 <ul>
 <li>In JavaScript, upgrade <code>@langchain/react</code>, <code>@langchain/vue</code>, <code>@langchain/svelte</code>, or <code>@langchain/angular</code> to 1.2.6 or later.</li>
@@ -1092,34 +1136,34 @@
 </ul>

-</ol><span data-as="p"><code>langchain</code> and <code>deepagents</code> accept any <code>langchain-core</code> 1.x, so upgrading either one can pull in 1.7.0. A call’s chunks concatenate into its full arguments, and <code>message.tool_calls.get()</code> still returns the parsed tool calls.</span></div></div></div>

 <li><strong>Typed answers</strong>: each interrupt carries a <code>response_schema</code> listing the decisions that call allows, so a client can build its form from the schema. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#read-the-response-schema" class="link">Read the response schema</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Checked before saving</strong>: LangGraph rejects an invalid answer before it is saved, so you can correct it and answer the same interrupt again. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#correct-an-invalid-answer" class="link">Correct an invalid answer</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Edits stay on the called tool</strong>: an <code>edit</code> can’t switch tools, and its arguments are checked against the tool’s own schema.</li>
 </ul><span data-as="p">Use per-call mode for new agents. LangChain plans to make it the default in the next major release. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#pause-once-per-tool-call" class="link">Pause once per tool call</a> and the <a href="/oss/python/concepts/human-in-the-loop" class="link">human-in-the-loop overview</a>.</span></div></div></div>

[&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The <code>langchain.mcp</code> namespace is in beta. Importing from it raises a <code>LangChainBetaWarning</code>.</span></div></div><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="features-2" aria-labelledby="_R_1c75crmhkd74t9_"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#features-2" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Features">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span id="_R_1c75crmhkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Features</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong>One adapter, any transport</strong>: <code>MCPAdapter</code> infers the transport from its target — a URL, a local script over stdio, an in-process server, an <code>MCPConfig</code> dict for <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/connections#multiple-servers" class="link">multiple servers</a>, or a pre-built <code>fastmcp.Client</code>. <code>await adapter.list_tools()</code> returns LangChain tools ready for <code>create_agent</code>. See the <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp" class="link">MCP overview</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Interrupt-driven elicitation</strong>: when a server asks for input mid-call, <code>MCPAdapter</code> surfaces the question as a LangGraph <code>interrupt()</code>, so a human answers and the run resumes. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#elicitation" class="link">Elicitation</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Authentication through FastMCP</strong>: bearer tokens, full OAuth 2.1 with dynamic client registration, or any <code>httpx.Auth</code>, including <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/auth#per-user-authentication" class="link">per-user authentication</a> in a deployment. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/auth" class="link">Authentication</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Richer tool metadata</strong>: each tool carries its MCP provenance under an <code>mcp</code> namespace on its metadata, including annotations such as <code>destructive_hint</code> for <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#human-in-the-loop" class="link">gating destructive tools behind approval</a>. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#tool-metadata" class="link">Tool metadata</a>.</li>
-</ul><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="migrating"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#migrating" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Migrating</span></h3><span data-as="p">Moving from <code>langchain-mcp-adapters</code>? <code>MultiServerMCPClient</code> is replaced by <code>MCPAdapter</code>, and several features have changed or been removed. See the <a href="/oss/python/migrate/langchain-mcp-adapters" class="link">migration guide</a>.</span></div></div></div>
-<div class="update flex flex-col relative items-start w-full lg:flex-row gap-2 lg:gap-6 py-8 update-container" id="jul-24-2026"><div class="lg:sticky top-(--scroll-mt) group flex flex-col w-full whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="langchain-core-v1-7-0"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#langchain-core-v1-7-0" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer"><code>langchain-core</code> v1.7.0</span></h2><span data-as="p">In <a href="/oss/python/langchain/event-streaming" class="link">event streaming</a>, tool-call arguments now stream as <code>tool-call-delta</code> events. Each event carries only the new part of the arguments, as <code>text-delta</code> does for text. Earlier versions sent <code>block-delta</code> events that repeated all of the arguments so far.</span><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="upgrade-clients-before-the-server"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#upgrade-clients-before-the-server" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Upgrade clients before the server</span></h3><span data-as="p">Clients older than the versions below do not handle <code>tool-call-delta</code>. For example, earlier versions of <code>@langchain/react</code> can stop updating a <code>useMessages</code> view.  <li>Upgrade every client that reads the stream:
 <ul>
 <li>In JavaScript, upgrade <code>@langchain/react</code>, <code>@langchain/vue</code>, <code>@langchain/svelte</code>, or <code>@langchain/angular</code> to 1.2.6 or later.</li>
@@ -1092,34 +1136,34 @@
 </ul>

-</ol><span data-as="p"><code>langchain</code> and <code>deepagents</code> accept any <code>langchain-core</code> 1.x, so upgrading either one can pull in 1.7.0. A call’s chunks concatenate into its full arguments, and <code>message.tool_calls.get()</code> still returns the parsed tool calls.</span></div></div></div>

 <li><strong>Typed answers</strong>: each interrupt carries a <code>response_schema</code> listing the decisions that call allows, so a client can build its form from the schema. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#read-the-response-schema" class="link">Read the response schema</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Checked before saving</strong>: LangGraph rejects an invalid answer before it is saved, so you can correct it and answer the same interrupt again. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#correct-an-invalid-answer" class="link">Correct an invalid answer</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Edits stay on the called tool</strong>: an <code>edit</code> can’t switch tools, and its arguments are checked against the tool’s own schema.</li>
 </ul><span data-as="p">Use per-call mode for new agents. LangChain plans to make it the default in the next major release. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/human-in-the-loop#pause-once-per-tool-call" class="link">Pause once per tool call</a> and the <a href="/oss/python/concepts/human-in-the-loop" class="link">human-in-the-loop overview</a>.</span></div></div></div>

[&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The <code>langchain.mcp</code> namespace is in beta. Importing from it raises a <code>LangChainBetaWarning</code>.</span></div></div><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="features-2" aria-labelledby="_R_1c75crmhkd74t9_"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#features-2" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Features">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span id="_R_1c75crmhkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Features</span></h3><ul>
 <li><strong>One adapter, any transport</strong>: <code>MCPAdapter</code> infers the transport from its target — a URL, a local script over stdio, an in-process server, an <code>MCPConfig</code> dict for <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/connections#multiple-servers" class="link">multiple servers</a>, or a pre-built <code>fastmcp.Client</code>. <code>await adapter.list_tools()</code> returns LangChain tools ready for <code>create_agent</code>. See the <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp" class="link">MCP overview</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Interrupt-driven elicitation</strong>: when a server asks for input mid-call, <code>MCPAdapter</code> surfaces the question as a LangGraph <code>interrupt()</code>, so a human answers and the run resumes. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#elicitation" class="link">Elicitation</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Authentication through FastMCP</strong>: bearer tokens, full OAuth 2.1 with dynamic client registration, or any <code>httpx.Auth</code>, including <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/auth#per-user-authentication" class="link">per-user authentication</a> in a deployment. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/auth" class="link">Authentication</a>.</li>
 <li><strong>Richer tool metadata</strong>: each tool carries its MCP provenance under an <code>mcp</code> namespace on its metadata, including annotations such as <code>destructive_hint</code> for <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#human-in-the-loop" class="link">gating destructive tools behind approval</a>. See <a href="/oss/python/langchain/mcp/tools#tool-metadata" class="link">Tool metadata</a>.</li>
-</ul><h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="migrating"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#migrating" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="2" class="size-3 shrink-0"><use href="#mi-mint-1-Link"></use></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Migrating</span></h3><span data-as="p">Moving from <code>langchain-mcp-adapters</code>? <code>MultiServerMCPClient</code> is replaced by <code>MCPAdapter</code>, and several features have changed or been removed. See the <a href="/oss/python/migrate/langchain-mcp-adapters" class="link">migration guide</a>.</span></div></div></div>

### [MODIFICADO] `langgraph::graph-api`
- **URL Canónica**: https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/graph-api
- **Hash Anterior**: `a433eb2486eae1701e16192d67923c59ba3b3ba8dfa36afb08a6cfc34273ca77`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `955c6053106ef1d2b147d1415d01381cc65aa8995b7a2ea0d6e1e95ac18dd7a8`

```diff
--- langgraph::graph-api (prev: a433eb24)
+++ langgraph::graph-api (new: 955c6053)
@@ -269,6 +269,21 @@
 You can add or remove nodes and edges without breaking resume for existing threads. Resumed runs use saved state and the graph you compile now.
 
 If a node calls [tasks](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#task) or [`interrupt`](https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/interrupt), stricter [determinism](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#determinism) rules apply on resume. Completed task results are restored from the checkpointer. Changing task or interrupt order before the resume point can mismatch cached values. See [Idempotency](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#idempotency).
+
+### Choose the unit of work
+
+A [node](#nodes) is a checkpoint and retry boundary. It is also where the graph can take a different path. Deterministic work such as parsing, retrieval, or a fixed tool chain does not need its own node unless you want that boundary.
+
+Ask whether the step decides what happens next, or executes something already decided. Then pick the primitive that matches the recovery behavior you need:
+
+| Primitive | Own checkpoint | On resume or retry | Choose when |
+| - | - | - | - |
+| Plain function inside a node | No. It runs inside the node's [super-step](#super-steps). | Re-runs whenever the node re-runs. | No routing fork after the step, and repeating it with the rest of the node is acceptable. |
+| [Task](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api.md#task) | No new graph checkpoint. The result is stored with the running entrypoint or parent checkpoint. | The enclosing entrypoint or node replays from the start. Completed tasks return stored results. | The step has a side effect or is expensive, and must not repeat on resume. |
+| [Subgraph](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-subgraphs.md) | Yes for stateful modes: nodes checkpoint under a namespace of the parent thread. Compile with `checkpointer=False` for no subgraph checkpoints. | Stateful modes resume inside the subgraph at the interrupted or failed node. | The unit has its own routing or state schema, or more than one parent graph reuses it. |
+| Graph node | Yes. A checkpoint follows the [super-step](#super-steps) it runs in. | Resume starts at that node. Earlier nodes do not re-run. | A routing fork is possible after the step, or you need an interrupt, resume, or [time travel](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/use-time-travel.md) boundary. |
+
+For side effects under retries, see [Retry boundaries and idempotency](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/fault-tolerance.md#retry-boundaries-and-idempotency). For task retries and timeouts, see [Tasks](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/functional-api-tasks.md).
 
 ### Using tasks in nodes
 
```
